## Q1-r1

arm `folder-nocode`, model `claude-haiku-5-5`, tools `Read,Grep,Glob`, effort `low`, wall 14.9 s

**Prompt** (from the .meta.json):

```
You are answering an actuarial question about Indiana's dig-damage enforcement record: the Indiana
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.

Frequency: across all five years, what percentage of cases have base section 16(h), and what percentage have base section 20(a)(2)? Round to one decimal.
```

```
You are a careful analyst. You can use the Read, Grep, Glob and Bash tools (Bash has Python 3). Your working directory is the only place you may look for files; use only files in it. Show the number or answer each question asks for in your final reply.
```

**Final answer** (verbatim):

```
**Answer: 16(h) = 39.6% and 20(a)(2) = 22.9%** of all cases (9,958 cases, 2021–2025).

- **Base section 16(h):** 3,941 cases ÷ 9,958 = 39.58%, so **39.6%**.
- **Base section 20(a)(2):** 2,284 cases ÷ 9,958 = 22.94%, so **22.9%**.

I got these from the pre-computed cube table (`reference/tables/02_cube.md`), summing every row for each base section across all qualifiers and both respondent types. Base section 16(i) is a separate section and is not included in 16(h).

Check: the cube rows sum to 9,958 cases, which matches the README and SCHEMA totals.

Caveats:
- Both duplicate-case issues are counted as separate cases, as the question defines a case as one form on one page. Counting the two identical pages of case 33669 once would change the denominator slightly, but not the rounded percentages.
- The 16 OCR-corrected cites are already reflected in the cube.
```

**Final answer** (verbatim):

```
**Answer:** 39.6% of cases have base section 16(h) (3,941 of 9,958), and 22.9% have base section 20(a)(2) (2,284 of 9,958).

**Reading taken:** All 9,958 case rows are the denominator, including the one row with a blank cite. Base sections are matched exactly as printed, so 16(i) is not counted with 16(h).

**Where from:** `reference/tables/01_overview_time.md`, table B (cases by base section x year). I checked both totals against the cube in `02_cube.md`, summing every row for each base section.

- 16(h): 756 + 1,101 + 811 + 803 + 470 = 3,941. 3,941 / 9,958 = 39.58%, so 39.6%.
- 20(a)(2): 340 + 586 + 497 + 482 + 379 = 2,284. 2,284 / 9,958 = 22.93%, so 22.9%.

**Caveat:** The 16(h) and 16(i) split is partly a lettering change. Damages in 2025 are cited as 16(i), so the 16(h) share is lower for later orders. The pooled figure above does not adjust for that.
```

```
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
```

```
**Source:** Table 03, S1 (by base section), which includes the qualified cites. For 16(h), S4 gives 3,941 cases across its five cite groups, and the zero and sum totals agree with S1. For 20(a)(2), S1 includes the single `20(a)(2)(expire)` case ($3,000), and S4 confirms the 2,284 total.

**Caveats:**
- The table's own `median_of_positive` is a median, not a mean, so I did not use it.
- The `$3, 500` row and the seven `$$1000` rows are read as the question defines them ($3,500 and $1,000). The tables use these values.
- The `.5000` and blank-recommendation rows have headline $0 and are excluded from the positive counts.
```

```
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:

  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
```

```
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.

  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".

  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:

  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
```

**Final answer** (verbatim):

```
**Answer**

- **Utility-respondent cases:** 1 of 2,715 have a warning letter, or **0.0%** (0.04% before rounding).
- **Non-utility-respondent cases:** 2,032 of 7,243 have a warning letter, or **28.1%** (28.05% before rounding).

**Where the numbers come from**

- Utility: `06_utility_types_otherparty.md`, table U3 (ALL utility row, W = 1/0) and U1 (ALL utility = 2,715 cases). The one warning letter is a Citizens Energy case.
- Non-utility: `06_utility_types_otherparty.md`, table N4, summing the W counts for the five keyword types (gov 18 + telecom 22 + energy_water 23 + contractor 319 + other 1,650 = 2,032), over N = 7,243 (table 01, row N).
- Cross-check: the warning-letter wording counts in `03_penalty_stats.md` (1,386 + 135 + 247 + 72 + 53 + 50 + 27 + 25 + 11 + 9 + 5 + 4 and others) add to about 2,033, which matches 2,032 + 1.

**Caveats**

- The tables classify outcomes by whether the text *starts with* "warning letter" (class W). They do not count "warning letter" appearing elsewhere in a recommendation. Class X is defined as having no warning or training wording, so it adds nothing. A recommendation such as "$500 or warning letter" would fall in class F or PT and would be missed. I could not check this from the tables, so the figures may slightly understate the true share.
- The non-utility split uses the keyword heuristic in table N, but that heuristic only affects the breakdown within non-utility cases. The utility/non-utility split itself is the PREAMBLE prefix test, which matches your definition.
```

```
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.
```

```
**Reading taken:** "Non-utility" is the table's N type, which the tables build from the 13-prefix test (06 U1 shows 2,715 utility cases, matching S2). Headline penalties follow the question's first-dollar rule as the tables apply it, with `$$1000` and `$3, 500` read as printed-intended amounts.

**2021 (order year), N cells from 03 S7, summed by base section:**

| Base | n | sum $ |
|---|---|---|
| 16(h) | 749 | 1,792,000 |
| 18(h) | 5 | 4,000 |
| 20(a)(1) | 103 | 206,500 |
| 20(a)(2) | 329 | 810,200 |
| 20(a)(3)(a) | 1 | 4,500 |
| 20(a)(3)(b) | 39 | 95,000 |
| **Total** | **1,226** | **2,912,200** |

Check: n matches O4 (2021 N = 1,226). Sum matches O4 headline columns (TR5 1,910,000 + PT 240,000 + F 762,200 = 2,912,200). 2,912,200 / 1,226 = 2,375.37, so **$2,375**.

**2025, N cells from 03 S7, summed by base section:**

| Base | n | sum $ |
|---|---|---|
| 16(h) | 468 | 1,283,500 |
| 16(i) | 239 | 685,500 |
| 18(h) | 5 | 9,000 |
| 18(j) | 4 | 4,000 |
| 19(c) | 4 | 2,000 |
| 20(a)(1) | 97 | 340,000 |
| 20(a)(2) | 363 | 1,252,000 |
| 20(a)(3)(a) | 5 | 19,500 |
| 20(a)(3)(b) | 42 | 135,500 |
| **Total** | **1,227** | **3,731,000** |

Check: n matches O4 (2025 N = 1,227). Sum matches O4 (TR5 2,055,000 + PT 410,000 + F 1,266,000 = 3,731,000). 3,731,000 / 1,227 = 3,040.75, so **$3,041**.

**Caveats:**
- The 2021 figure includes one known-bad row, ord_PSD_020321.pdf page 27 (case "0", recommendation ".5000", headline $0). The SCHEMA says to treat it as unusable for case-level counts. Excluding it gives 2,912,200 / 1,225 = $2,377.
- The 2025 N cells carry no known defects.
- The 16(h)/16(i) and 18(h)/18(j) letter changes do not affect these year totals, since both lettering schemes are summed within each year.
```

```
**Answer: 428 cases contain "ticket escalation", and the most common headline penalty among them is $1,500 (284 cases).**

Source: `reference/tables/03_penalty_stats.md`, section R (recommendation text variants, all years). I summed the rows whose text contains "ticket escalation" (every one of them is "no ticket escalation - additional $1,000" or a variant):

| Cases | Headline | Outcome | Text |
|---|---|---|---|
| 268 | $1,500 | PT | $1,500, respondent can complete training instead of paying $1,500; no ticket escalation - additional |
| 52 | $3,000 | F | $3,000; no ticket escalation - additional $1,000 |
| 46 | $5,000 | F | $5,000; no ticket escalation - additional $1,000 |
| 22 | $3,500 | F | $3,500; no ticket escalation - additional $1,000 |
| 14 | $4,000 | F | $4,000; no ticket escalation - additional $1,000 |
| 10 | $4,500 | F | $4,500; no ticket escalation - additional $1,000 |
| 8 | $1,500 | PT | $1,500; no ticket escalation - additional $1,000. Respondent may complete training… |
| 8 | $1,500 | PT | $1,500; no ticket escalation-pay additional $1,000. Respondent may complete training… |

Total: 268 + 52 + 46 + 22 + 14 + 10 + 8 + 8 = **428**.

Headline counts: $1,500 = 268 + 8 + 8 = **284**, the most common. The next are $3,000 (52) and $5,000 (46).

Caveats:
- **Reading taken:** "ticket escalation" is matched as a substring, so "no ticket escalation" counts. Under a stricter reading that excludes "no ticket", the count would be zero, so I took the literal substring reading.
- **Possible undercount:** the table pools all texts with fewer than 3 cases (94 cases in total) without showing their wording. Any "ticket escalation" wording among them is not counted, so 428 is a floor. The shares in the pool are not broken out, so I cannot say how many of the 94 they include.
- **Not counted:** the "concealment escalation" and "self-repair attempt escalation" texts do not contain "ticket escalation".
- **Different table:** the "escalat" count of 20 in table 07 (section K) is from the additional_info notes, not the recommendation text, so I did not use it.
- **Year:** the table gives all years combined, so the 428 is not split by order year.
```

```
**Answer: "Northern Indiana Public Service Company" is the most frequent other party among non-utility-respondent cases. It appears in 3,015 of 7,243 cases, or 41.6%.**

**Denominator:** 7,243 non-utility-respondent cases. Table 06 section P gives 6,948 + 268 + 27 = 7,243 for respondent type N. This also equals 9,958 total cases − 2,715 utility-respondent cases.

**Numerator:** Table 06 section P3 lists "Northern Indiana Public Service Company" in 3,068 cases in total, of which 53 have a utility respondent. That leaves 3,068 − 53 = 3,015 non-utility-respondent cases.

**Check against other names:**
- The next name, "CenterPoint Energy Indiana", has 2,553 − 64 = 2,489 non-utility cases.
- Names outside the top 25 have at most 14 cases each, so none can exceed 3,015.

**Calculation:** 3,015 / 7,243 = 41.63%, which rounds to **41.6%**.

**Caveat:** The utility split in P3 uses the respondent-prefix rule, which matches the question's utility definition. I did not have the raw table, so I did not re-verify the 53 figure from the case rows.
```

```
You are answering an actuarial question about Indiana's dig-damage enforcement record: the Indiana
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.
```

```
Expired tickets: among cases with base section 16(h), what percentage have the qualifier "expire", and what percentage have the qualifier "prior"? Round to one decimal.
```

**Final answer** (verbatim):

```
**Answers (order-year basis):**
- **2023 mean lag: 227 days** (227.3 from table G, n = 2,107)
- **2024 mean lag: 188 days** (187.7 from table G, n = 1,897)
- **Share of cases with lag > 365 days: 12.6%** (1,259 of 9,957 cases with parseable dates, all years)

**Reading taken:** lag = calendar days from damage date to order date, grouped by order year. "More than 365 days" is read as 366 days or more.

**Where from:**
- Means: `reference/tables/01_overview_time.md`, section G, "damage->order mean" column. Rounded to whole days at the end.
- Percentage: section G3 ("Damage->order lag buckets by order year"). Lags over 365 days are the 366-730d bucket plus the >730d bucket, summed over all five years:
  - 366-730d: 1,000 + 185 + 21 + 31 + 13 = 1,250
  - >730d: 6 + 0 + 3 + 0 + 0 = 9
  - Total: 1,259
- Denominator: the G3 bucket counts sum to 9,957 (1,599 + 2,670 + 2,107 + 1,897 + 1,684), matching the "all" row of table G. 1,259 / 9,957 = 12.645%, so 12.6%.

**Caveats:**
- The means come from a pre-computed table; I could not recompute them from the raw case table, which is not in this folder.
- One row has a blank damage date and drops out of the lag figures. That is why the denominator is 9,957 and not 9,958.
```

```
You are answering an actuarial question about Indiana's dig-damage enforcement record: the Indiana
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.

Abstentions: a case has a **recorded abstention** if its Abstentions field has any text left after trimming spaces and deleting any stray words "Additional Infonnation:" or "Additional Information:" that leaked into it. What percentage of cases in each of 2021, 2024 and 2025 (by order year) have a recorded abstention? Round each to one decimal.
```

**Final answer** (verbatim):

```
**Recorded-abstention rates by order year**

| Year | Cases | Blank abstentions | Recorded abstention | Percentage |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2021 | 1,599 | 936 | 662 | **41.4%** |
| 2024 | 1,897 | 872 | 1,025 | **54.0%** |
| 2025 | 1,684 | 846 | 838 | **49.8%** |

**Sources:** Case totals and blank counts come from table 01 (A) and table 07 (M), in `reference/tables/`.

**Adjustments:**
- In 2021, one row reads only "Additional Infonnation:", so after removing the stray text it is empty and is not counted. The other leaked row, "Smith Additional Infonnation:", still has the name "Smith" and is counted.
- The data shows "Infonnation" (OCR spelling). I treated it as the same stray phrase.
- Row checks: the non-blank abstention counts summed from table M match the case totals minus blanks for each year (2021: 663 before the adjustment, 2024: 1,025, 2025: 838).

These figures come from pre-computed tables, not the raw case table, so they rest on the tables' own cleaning.
```

**Final answer** (verbatim):

```
**Answers (by order year, recorded abstention = Abstentions field has text after removing stray "Additional Information:" labels):**

- **2021: 41.4%** (662 of 1,599 cases)
- **2024: 54.0%** (1,025 of 1,897 cases)
- **2025: 49.8%** (838 of 1,684 cases)

**Where the numbers come from**

- **Denominators:** table 01, section A (year summary), gives 1,599, 1,897 and 1,684 cases for 2021, 2024 and 2025.
- **Blank abstentions:** table 07, section M, gives 936 (2021), 872 (2024) and 846 (2025) cases with a blank field.
- **Recorded abstentions:** denominator minus blanks, less stray-label-only rows.
  - 2021: 1,599 − 936 − 1 = 662. The one row reading only "Additional Infonnation:" is excluded because nothing remains after the label is removed. The "Smith Additional Infonnation:" row is kept, since "Smith" remains.
  - 2024: 1,897 − 872 = 1,025.
  - 2025: 1,684 − 846 = 838.
- **Check:** each year's abstention-text counts in table M sum to that year's case total in table A.

**Caveats**

- The label is spelled "Infonnation" (with an "n") in the table, the OCR form of "Information". I treated both spellings as stray labels.
- I took the blank count from table M as the number of cases with no abstention text. I did not check the raw data for whitespace-only fields, which the table would already count as blank.
```

## H3-r3

arm `folder-nocode`, model `claude-haiku-5-5`, tools `Read,Grep,Glob`, effort `low`, wall 13.3 s

```
You are answering an actuarial question about Indiana's dig-damage enforcement record: the Indiana
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.

Abstentions: a case has a **recorded abstention** if its Abstentions field has any text left after trimming spaces and deleting any stray words "Additional Infonnation:" or "Additional Information:" that leaked into it. What percentage of cases in each of 2021, 2024 and 2025 (by order year) have a recorded abstention? Round each to one decimal.
```

```
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".

  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.
```
